, Formula (
125
) is a version of
[
McL19
, Corollary 1.4]
. While loc. cit. uses the graded abstract contact open book arising from
f/|f|
restricted to a Milnor sphere, that is, the Milnor fibration (
2
) in the sphere, we use the graded abstract contact open book associated with the Milnor fibration (
3
) in the tube, constructed in Example
5.22
.

Remark 8.4
.

Theorem
1.1
implies an analogous statement in a purely algebraic setting. More precisely, let
{k}
be a field of characteristic zero, and let
f\in{k}[t][\![z_{1},\dots,z_{n}]\!]
. Write
f_{t}=f(t,\cdot)\in{k}[\![z_{1},\dots,z_{n}]\!]
; so
t\mapsto f_{t}
is an algebraic family of power series. Assume that the Milnor number of
f_{t}
is finite and independent of
t
. Then the multiplicity of
f_{t}
is independent of
t
, too.

Indeed, like in the proof of Lemma
8.1
(a)
, we first replace
f
by its truncation
\sum_{|I|<N}a_{I}z^{I}
, for some
a_{I}\in{k}[t]
and an integer
N
independent of
t
, without changing the multiplicity and the Milnor number of each
f_{t}
. Let now
{k}_{0}
be a field generated over
\mathbb{Q}
by all coefficients of polynomials
a_{I}
; so
a_{I}\in{k}_{0}[t]
for all
I
. Since
{k}_{0}
is a finitely generated extension of
\mathbb{Q}
, we have an embedding
{k}_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{C}
. This way,
(f_{t})
becomes a
\mu
-constant family of isolated complex hypersurface singularities, and Theorem
1.1
shows that the multiplicity of
f_{t}
does not depend on
t
, as claimed.

We recall that Question
1.2
, i.e. the original Zariski Question A, does not require the hypersurface singularities to be isolated. Corollary
8.5
below, which was proved and communicated to us by David Massey, summarizes some direct consequences of our results for non-isolated singularities.

In this setting, a proper analogue of the Milnor number is the sequence of
Lê numbers
, introduced in
[
Mas90
, Definition 2.1]
. Thus a possible generalization of Theorem
1.1
to this setting is given by Corollary
8.5
(a)
. However, since Lê numbers are not a topological invariant
[
FdB05
, Theorem A(i)]
, Corollary
8.5
(a)
does not imply the positive answer to (a family version of) Question
1.2
.

Nonetheless,
[
Mas95
, Theorem 7.9]
implies a positive answer in the special case of aligned singularities, see Corollary
8.5
(b)
. A germ of a hypersurface singularity
f\colon(\mathbb{C}^{n},0)\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C},0)
is
aligned
, see
[
Mas95
, Definition 7.1]
, if its singular locus admits a stratification satisfying the Thom
a_{f}
condition, such that the closure of each stratum is smooth at the origin.

Corollary 8.5

(Massey)
.

Let
f_{t}\colon(\mathbb{C}^{n},0)\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C},0)
be a continuous family of germs of hypersurface singularities, possibly non-isolated. Assume that
(f_{t})
satisfies one of the following conditions.

(a)

For some coordinate system on
\mathbb{C}^{n}
, all Lê numbers of
f_{t}
are independent of
t
.

(b)

The embedded topological type of
f_{t}
is independent of
t
, and each
f_{t}
has aligned singularities.

Then the multiplicity of
f_{t}
is independent of
t
.

(a)
Let
(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})
be the chosen coordinate system. Since multiplicity is upper semicontinuous, there is
N\geqslant 0
such that
\nu(f_{t})\leqslant N
for all
t
. Applying inductively the uniform Lê–Iomdine formulas
[
Mas95
, Theorem 4.5]
, we conclude that for some
j>N
,
k\in\{0,\dots,n\}
, and
a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
, the singularity
g_{t}\coloneqq f_{t}+a_{1}z_{1}^{j}+\dots+a_{k}z_{k}^{j}
is isolated, and its sequence of Lê numbers – which in this case consists just of the Milnor number
\mu(g_{t})
, see
[
Mas95
, Example 2.1]
– is independent of
t
. By Theorem
1.1
, the multiplicity of
g_{t}
is independent of
t
. Since
j>N\geqslant\nu(f_{t})
, we conclude that
\nu(f_{t})=\nu(g_{t})
, so the family
(f_{t})
is equimultiple, too, as needed.

(b)
Replacing
f_{t}
by the family
\widetilde{f}_{t}\colon(\mathbb{C}^{n+k},0)\longrightarrow(\mathbb{C},0)
given by
\widetilde{f}_{t}(z,w)=f_{t}(z)
, we can assume that
n\geqslant 4
. Now, part
(b)
follows from
[
Mas95
, Theorem 7.9]
and Corollary
1.3
.

∎

We conclude with an example of two isolated hypersurface singularities
f_{0},f_{1}\in\mathbb{C}[\![x,y,z,w]\!]
which are topologically equivalent, but are not connected by a
\mu
-constant family. The existence of such examples shows that Question
1.2
, i.e. the original Zariski Question A, is strictly stronger than Theorem
1.1
. This example arose during our discussions with Norbert A’Campo and Enrique Artal.

Example 8.6

(cf.
[
ABCN+02
]
)
.

Let
g_{0},g_{1}\in\mathbb{C}[\![x,y]\!]
be two curve germs with the same integral Seifert form but different embedded topological type. A series of such pairs was constructed in
[
DBM94
]
, a particular example is given by

\begin{split}g_{0}&=((y^{2}-x^{3})^{2}-x^{17}-4yx^{10})((x^{2}-y^{5})^{2}-y^{11}-4xy^{8}),\\
g_{1}&=((y^{2}-x^{3})^{2}-x^{15}-4yx^{9})((x^{2}-y^{5})^{2}-y^{13}-4xy^{9}).\end{split}

For
i\in\{0,1\}
let
f_{i}\coloneqq g_{i}+z^{2}+w^{2}\in\mathbb{C}[\![x,y,z,w]\!]
be the double suspension of
g_{i}
. By
[
Sak74
, Theorem 2]
, the links of
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
have the same Seifert form. Results of Kervaire and Levine, see
[
Dur74
]
, imply that
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
are topologically equivalent.

Suppose
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
occur in a
\mu
-constant family
(f_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
. By Theorem
1.1
, each
f_{t}
has multiplicity
2
. Applying Morse lemma in families, cf.
[
GLS07
, Theorem 2.47]
, we infer that in some local coordinates
(z_{1},\dots,z_{4})
we have
f_{t}=z_{1}^{2}+\check{f}_{t}
for some
\mu
-constant family
(\check{f}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}\subset\mathbb{C}[\![z_{2},z_{3},z_{4}]\!]
. Since
\check{f}_{0}
,
\check{f}_{1}
have multiplicity two, once again applying Theorem
1.1
and Morse lemma we get, after a coordinate change, a
\mu
-constant family
(\check{g}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}\subset\mathbb{C}[\![z_{3},z_{4}]\!]
such that
f_{t}=z_{1}^{2}+z_{2}^{2}+\check{g}_{t}
and
\check{g}_{i}=g_{i}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
. By
[
LR76
]
, the family
(\check{g}_{t})
is topologically trivial, so
g_{0}
is topologically equivalent to
g_{1}
, a contradiction.
